0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern 0.17224pt\}

and
\kern 0.17224pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern 0.17224pt\leq\mathsf{ws}
.
Then
\kern 0.17224pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern 0.17224pt\prec_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\kern 0.17224pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern 0.17224pt\prec_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}t
for every
t\in\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak\emptyset)\setminus\{\kern 0.17224pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\top$}\kern 0.17224pt,\kern 0.17224pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\kern-0.08612pt\raise 0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise 0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.08612pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern-7.7778pt\raise-0.17224pt\hbox{$\bot$}\kern 0.17224pt\}
.

Theorem 20
.

Let
\mathsf{diff}\leq\mathsf{ws}
.
For all ground types
\tau,\upsilon
and ground preterms
s,t,u:\tau\to\upsilon
,
we have
u\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}u\>\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
.

Proof 3.14
.

Since
u
is of type
\tau\to\upsilon
, in its
\eta
-long normal form, it has the form
\lambda\>u^{\prime}
for some
u^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
is a symbol, we can obtain
the
\eta
-long
\beta
-normal form of
u\>\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)

by replacing the free De Bruijn indices of
u^{\prime}
by

\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
.

So,
in order to show that
u\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}u\>\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
,
we apply rule
3a
,
and it remains to show that
u^{\prime}\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}u\>\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
.
We follow the structure of
u^{\prime}

and
u\>\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
as follows.
Whenever heads coincide,
we apply rules
1d
,

2c
, or
3c

to decompose both sides.
When heads do not coincide, we note that by our observation above,
this can only happen when one side is a De Bruijn index
and the other side is

\mathsf{diff}\langle\tau,\upsilon\rangle(s,t)
.
So we can then apply rule
2d

because
\mathsf{diff}\leq\mathsf{ws}
.
For any
\cal{chkargs}
conditions arising in this procedure,
we apply rules
1a
,

2a
, or
3a
,
and use the same procedure for the resulting proof obligations.

The above proof crucially depends on
\mathsf{diff}
’s placement below the
watershed. If we allowed
\mathsf{diff}>\mathsf{ws}
, the comparison
0\succ_{\mathsf{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\mathsf{diff}(\ldots)
would fail. Theorem
20
is the
watershed’s reason for being.

4.
The Monomorphic Level

Next, we generalize the definition of
\lambda
KBO to monomorphic nonground
preterms: preterms containing no type variables. The result coincides with the ground

\lambda
KBO on ground preterms while supporting term variables. Variables give
rise to polynomial constraints, which must be solved when comparing terms.

The key idea, already present in the
\lambda
-free KBO by Becker et al.
[
1
]
, is to use polynomials to symbolically represent the
weight of a nonground term. The weight of
y\>\mathsf{a}
, where
\mathsf{a}:\kappa
, will be represented symbolically as
1+\mathbf{w}_{y}+\mathbf{k}_{y,1}(\cal w(\mathsf{a})-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})
, where
1+\mathbf{w}_{y}

stands for the weight of whatever term will instantiate
y
without its leading

\lambda
s and
\mathbf{k}_{y,1}
for the number of copies of the first curried
argument, here
\mathsf{a}
, that the term will make. If an argument coefficient
other than
1
is used, that number of copies will be inflated by the
coefficient. The
{-}\>\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}
monomial accounts for the loss of a De
Bruijn index occurring in
y
when passing the argument
\mathsf{a}
and

\beta
-reducing.

A subtle difference between the indeterminate
\mathbf{k}_{y,1}
and the
argument coefficient
\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)
is that
\mathbf{k}_{y,1}
can take a
value of
0
; for example,
\lambda\>\mathsf{b}
makes zero copies of its
argument. Becker et al. excluded this scenario so that they could get
compatibility with arguments, but this property is not needed by

\lambda
-superposition.

Another subtlety concerns higher-order functions. The arithmetic above works
because the argument
\mathsf{a}
is a simple symbol. If it were a

\lambda
-abstraction, it could appear applied inside
y
and trigger further

\beta
-reductions, complicating matters. In such cases, we simply give up and
use a single indeterminate
\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}}
to represent both the
applied variable and its arguments of functional types. We do the same with
arguments of variable type, since type variables can be instantiated with
functional types.

Some precision can be gained by normalizing the subscript of

\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}}
. For example, if
\mathsf{a}
and
\mathsf{b}
have the same
weight, then
\mathbf{w}_{y\>(\lambda\>\mathsf{a}\>0)}
and

\mathbf{w}_{y\>(\lambda\>\mathsf{b}\>0)}
will always evaluate to the same
result and can be identified. Our simple analysis merges all symbols and De
Bruijn indices with the same weight using a normalization function
\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\phantom{i}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}
.

4.1.
\bm{\lambda}
KBO

{defi}

Let
(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\Sigma)
be a higher-order signature.
We denote by
\mathbf{P}
the set of

\mathbf{O}
-valued polynomials of the following distinct indeterminates,
where
y\in X
,
\bar{t}\in(\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak X))^{*}
, and

i\in\mathbb{N}_{>0}
:

–

\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}}
, ranging over
\mathbf{O}
, represents the weight, minus
1, of the variable
y
applied to the arguments
\bar{t}
but without
any leading
\lambda
s corresponding to extra arguments;

–

\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t},i}
, ranging over
\mathbf{O}
, represents the coefficient
to apply on the
i
th extra argument of
y
already applied to
\bar{t}
.

An
assignment
is a mapping from indeterminates to values in the
indeterminates’ specified ranges. Given a polynomial
\cal w
and an assignment

A
,
\cal w{\big|}_{A}\in\mathbf{O}
denotes
\cal w
’s value under
A
, obtained by
replacing each indeterminate
\mathbf{x}
in
A
’s domain by
A(\mathbf{x})
.
Overloading notation, we write
\cal w{\big|}_{\sigma}
for
the application of the polynomial substitution
\sigma
to
\cal w
;
for example, if
\sigma=\{\mathbf{w}_{y}\mapsto\mathbf{w}_{z}\}
, then

\mathbf{w}_{y}{\big|}_{\sigma}=\mathbf{w}_{z}
.
Given polynomials
w,w^{\prime}
, we write
w^{\prime}\geq w
if we have
w^{\prime}{\big|}_{A}\geq w{\big|}_{A}
for every assignment
A
, and similarly for
>
,
\leq
,
<
, and
=
.

{defi}

Let
\Sigma^{\prime}=\Sigma\uplus\{k_{\tau}\mid k\in\mathbf{O}_{>0}\text{ and }\tau\in\cal{Ty}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\emptyset)\}
.
Define the normalization function
\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\phantom{i}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}:\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak X)\to\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma^{\prime},\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak X)

recursively by

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}y\>\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=y\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}k_{\tau}\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}&\text{if $\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)=1$ for every $i$, with $\cal w(\mathsf{f})=k$ and $\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}:\tau$}\\
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}m\langle\tau\rangle\>\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=(\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})_{\tau}\>\bm{[\kern 0.83328pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.83328pt]}

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\lambda\>t\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=\lambda\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}t\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

{defi}

Let
\cal w:\Sigma\to\mathbf{O}_{>0}
,
\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}\in\mathbf{O}_{>0}
,
and
\cal k:\Sigma\times\mathbb{N}_{>0}\to\mathbf{O}_{>0}
.
Given a list of preterms
\bar{t}
, let
\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}
denote the longest suffix
consisting of steady preterms, and let
\bar{t}^{\>\star}

denote the complementary prefix.
Define the monomorphic weight function
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}:\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak X)\to\mathbf{P}
recursively by

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(y\>\bar{t})

\displaystyle=1+\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bm{[\kern 0.67078pt}\bar{t}^{\>\star}\bm{\kern 0.67078pt]}}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{|\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}|}\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bm{[\kern 0.67078pt}\bar{t}^{\>\star}\bm{\kern 0.67078pt]},i}(\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}_{i})-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}_{n})

\displaystyle=\cal w(\mathsf{f})+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i})

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(m\>\bar{t}_{n})

\displaystyle=\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i})

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\lambda\>t)

\displaystyle=\cal w_{\lambda}+\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)

In the first equation,
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}_{i})
gives the argument’s weight,
whereas
\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}
is the weight of the
De Bruijn index that gets replaced by the argument.

Remark 21
.

It is possible to generalize the theory above to let
\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}
consist of
all steady preterms, regardless of their location. The interpretation of

\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}}
and
\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t},i}
must then be changed to
shuffle the
\lambda
s, pulling those corresponding to the arguments
\bar{t}
to
the front. For example, if
y:\kappa\to(\kappa\to\kappa)\to\kappa
,
the indeterminate
\mathbf{w}_{y\>t}
represents the weight of the term

(\lambda\>\lambda\>y\>0\>1)\>t=\lambda\>y\>0\>(t{\uparrow})
(but without its
leading
\lambda
).

Another possible generalization would be to normalize complex preterms, producing
for example
\mathsf{2}\langle\tau\rangle
instead of

\mathsf{1}\langle\sigma\to\tau\rangle\>\mathsf{1}\langle\sigma\rangle
.

{defi}

Let
\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}},\cal w,\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}},\cal k,\allowbreak\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

be as in Definition
4.1
.
Let
>^{\mathsf{ty}}
be a precedence on
\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}}
.
Let
\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
be the strict first-order KBO on
\cal T(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\emptyset)

induced by
\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}}
and
>^{\mathsf{ty}}
.
Let
>
be a precedence on
\Sigma
.

The
strict monomorphic
\lambda
KBO

\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

and the
nonstrict monomorphic
\lambda
KBO

\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
induced by
\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}},\cal w,\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}},\cal k,\allowbreak>^{\mathsf{ty}},>
on

\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak X)

are defined by mutual induction.
The strict
relation is defined so that

t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
if any
of these conditions is met:

(1)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
;

(2)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
,

t
is of the form
\lambda\langle\upsilon\rangle\>t^{\prime}
, and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

s
is of the form
\lambda\langle\tau\rangle\>s^{\prime}
and

\upsilon\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}\tau
, or

(b)

s
is of the form
\lambda\langle\upsilon\rangle\>s^{\prime}

and
t^{\prime}\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s^{\prime}
, or

(c)

s
is of the form
m\>\bar{s}
or

\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
;

(3)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
,
t
is of the form

n\>\bar{t}
, and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

s
is of the form
m\>\bar{s}
and
n>m
, or

(b)

s
is of the form
n\>\bar{s}
and

\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}\bar{s}
, or

(c)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
;

(4)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
,
t
is of the form

\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\upsilon}\rangle(\bar{w})\>\bar{t}
, and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
and

\mathsf{g}>\mathsf{f}
, or

(b)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\tau}\rangle(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
and

\bar{\upsilon}\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{ty}}\bar{\tau}
, or

(c)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\upsilon}\rangle(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
and

(\bar{w},\bar{t})\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}(\bar{u},\bar{s})
.

The nonstrict
relation is defined so that

t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
if any
of these conditions is met:

(1)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
;

(2)

t
is of the form
y\>\bar{t}
,
s
is of the form
y\>\bar{s}
, and for
every
i
,
t_{i}
is steady and
t_{i}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s_{i}
;

(3)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
,

t
is of the form
\lambda\langle\upsilon\rangle\>t^{\prime}
, and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

s
is of the form
\lambda\langle\tau\rangle\>s^{\prime}
and

\upsilon\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}\tau
, or

(b)

s
is of the form
\lambda\langle\upsilon\rangle\>s^{\prime}

and
t^{\prime}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s^{\prime}
, or

(c)

s
is of the form
m\>\bar{s}
or

\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
;

(4)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
,
t
is of the form

n\>\bar{t}
, and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

s
is of the form
m\>\bar{s}
and
n>m
, or

(b)

s
is of the form
n\>\bar{s}
and

\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}\bar{s}
, or

(c)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
;

(5)

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
,
t
is of the form

\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\upsilon}\rangle(\bar{w})\>\bar{t}
, and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
and

\mathsf{g}>\mathsf{f}
, or

(b)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\tau}\rangle(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
and

\bar{\upsilon}\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{ty}}\bar{\tau}
, or

(c)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\upsilon}\rangle(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
and

(\bar{w},\bar{t})\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}(\bar{u},\bar{s})
.

Rules
2
to
4
for
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and rules

3
to
5
for
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
use
\geq
instead
of
=
to compare weights because polynomials cannot always be compared
precisely. For example, if
\cal w(\mathsf{a})=1
, where
\mathsf{a}:\kappa
, we can
know that
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(x)=1+\mathbf{w}_{x}\geq 1=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathsf{a})
even though
neither
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(x)>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathsf{a})
nor
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(x)=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathsf{a})
.

To determine whether one preterm is larger than another, we must solve an
inequality, which can be recast into
w\geq 0
or
w>0
. The strict case
arises in rule
1
of the definitions of
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and

\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
. The two cases can be unified by writing
w>0
as
w-1\geq 0
.
Solving systems of integer polynomial inequalities is in general undecidable.
Here, however, we have a single polynomial
w
, in which indeterminates range
only over nonnegative values. This is the key to solving the problem efficiently
in practice. If infinite ordinals (e.g.
\omega
) are used as the weight or
coefficient associated with any symbols, we must also let the

\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}}
and
\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t},i}
indeterminates range over
these.

Specifically, we propose the following procedure to check an inequality of the
above form: Put
w
in standard form. If all monomial coefficients are
nonnegative, report that the inequality holds. Otherwise, report that it might
not hold.
This simple procedure can lose solutions. For example,

(\mathbf{w}_{y}-3)\mathbf{w}_{y}+3\geq 0
holds, yet its standard form

\mathbf{w}_{y}^{2}-3\mathbf{w}_{y}+3
contains a negative coefficient, which is
enough to lead the procedure astray.

Below we will connect the monomorphic
\lambda
KBO with its ground counterpart
to lift its properties.

Lemma 22
.

For all preterms
t
, we have
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
.

Proof 4.1
.

By structural induction on
t
, using rules
3b
,
4b
, and
5c
.

Lemma 23
.

If
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, then
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

Proof 4.2
.

By induction on the derivation of
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.
For each rule, there is a clearly corresponding rule of
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
to apply.
The induction hypothesis is only required for applying rule
3b
.
A crucial observation is that
\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
implies
\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
by definition.

{defi}

Let
\theta
be a substitution
that maps to terms containing only nonfunctional variables.
We define an assignment
\cal{poly}(\theta)
that
maps each indeterminate
\mathbf{x}
to a value according to the semantics given
by Definition
4.1
after applying
\theta
onto the
considered preterms.
We define

\cal{poly}(\theta)(\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}})=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}((y\>\bar{t})\theta!)-1
,
where, given a preterm
t
,
t!
denotes the same preterm without any leading

\lambda
s—e.g.,
(\lambda\>\lambda\>\mathsf{f}\>0)!=\mathsf{f}\>0
.
As for

\cal{poly}(\theta)(\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t},i})
,
it is defined as the number of De Bruijn indices in
(y\>\bar{t})\theta
referring to its

i
th argument, multiplied by all argument coefficients above it.
Here, it is crucial that
\theta
maps to terms containing only nonfunctional variables
because the argument coefficient to assign to a variable is not always clear.

Lemma 24
.

Given a substitution
\theta
that maps to terms containing only nonfunctional variables, we have

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{\cal{poly}(\theta)}=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t\theta)
.

Proof 4.3
.

The proof is by induction on the definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}
. Let
A=\cal{poly}(\theta)
.

Case
t=y\>\bar{t}
:
We have

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(y\>\bar{t}){\big|}_{A}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle 1+\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}^{\>\star}}{\big|}_{A}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{\smash{|\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}|}}\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t}^{\>\star},i}{\big|}_{A}(\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}_{i}){\big|}_{A}-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}
and
\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\phantom{i}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle 1+\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}^{\>\star}}{\big|}_{A}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{|\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}|}\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t}^{\>\star},i}{\big|}_{A}(\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}_{i}\theta)-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})

by the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}((y\>\bar{t})\theta)

by the semantics of
\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{k}

In the last step, the arithmetic works because all preterms in
\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}
are
steady. This means that they will not trigger any
\beta
-reductions when they replace
a De Bruijn index.

Case
t=\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}_{n}
:
We have

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}_{n}){\big|}_{A}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal w(\mathsf{f})+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i}){\big|}_{A}

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal w(\mathsf{f})+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i}\theta)

by the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\mathsf{f}(\bar{u}\theta)\>(\bar{t}_{n}\theta))

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}((\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}_{n})\theta)

by definition of substitution

Case
t=m\>\bar{t}_{n}
:
This case is similar to the previous one.
We have

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(m\>\bar{t}_{n}){\big|}_{A}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i}){\big|}_{A}

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i}\theta)

by the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(m\>(\bar{t}_{n}\theta))

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}((m\>\bar{t}_{n})\theta)

by definition of substitution

Case
t=\lambda\>t
:
We have

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\lambda\>t){\big|}_{A}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal w_{\lambda}+\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{A}

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal w_{\lambda}+\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t\theta)

by the induction hypothesis

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(\lambda\>(t\theta))

by definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}

\displaystyle{=}\;

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}((\lambda\>t)\theta)

by definition of substitution

The nonground relation
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
underapproximates the ground relation
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

in the following sense:

Theorem 25
.

If
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, then
t\theta\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s\theta

for all grounding substitutions
\theta
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, then
t\theta\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s\theta

for all grounding substitutions
\theta
.

Proof 4.4
.

We prove both claims by
mutual induction on the shape of the derivation of
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s

and
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.
Let
A=\cal{poly}(\theta)
.

First,
we make the following observation:
For all tuples of preterms
\bar{t}
and
\bar{s}
covered by the induction hypothesis,

–

\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}\bar{s}
implies
\bar{t}\theta\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}\bar{s}\theta
, and

–

\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}\bar{s}
implies

\bar{t}\theta\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}\bar{s}\theta
or

\bar{t}\theta=\bar{s}\theta
.

This follows from the induction hypothesis and the definitions of the lexicographic extensions (Definitions
2.2
,
2.2
, and
2.2
)
by induction on the length of the tuples.

With this observation, we prove the two claims of this theorem as follows.
For the first claim, we
make a case distinction on the rule deriving
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
:

Rule
1
:
From
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
, we have
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{A}>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s){\big|}_{A}
,
and by Lemma
24
, we get

\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t\theta)>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s\theta)
.
By definition of
W_{\mathsf{g}}
and
W_{\mathsf{m}}
, they coincide on ground preterms,
and thus
\cal W_{\mathsf{g}}(t\theta)>\cal W_{\mathsf{g}}(s\theta)
.
So, rule
1
of
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
applies.

Rules
2
,
3
,
4
:
We have
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
. If
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{A}>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s){\big|}_{A}
,
rule
1
applies as above. Otherwise,
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{A}=\cal W(s){\big|}_{A}
, and the corresponding rule
2
,
3
,
or
4
applies. The only mismatches between the two definitions are
the use of
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
versus
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and

\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
versus
\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
.
These are repaired by the induction
hypothesis and our observation above.

For the second claim, we
make a case distinction on the rule deriving
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
:

Rule
1
:
As for
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
above.

Rule
2
:
We will focus on the case where the argument lists
\bar{t}
and
\bar{s}
have
length 1 and the corresponding De Bruijn index in
y\theta
occurs exactly once.
The same line of reasoning can be repeated for further arguments or further De
Bruijn index occurrences by appealing to the transitivity of
\succsim_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
.

Since
t_{1}
is of nonfunctional type,
(y\>t_{1})\theta
and
(y\>s_{1})\theta
must be of the forms
t^{\prime}=u\begin{picture}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{45.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{315.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}\!\!\begin{picture}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{45.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{315.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}\,t_{1}\theta{\uparrow}^{k}\begin{picture}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{135.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{225.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}\!\!\begin{picture}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{135.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{225.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}
and
s^{\prime}=u\begin{picture}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{45.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{315.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}\!\!\begin{picture}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{45.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(1.1,2.5){\rotatebox{315.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}\,s_{1}\theta{\uparrow}^{k}\begin{picture}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{135.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{225.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}\!\!\begin{picture}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{135.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\put(0.0,2.5){\rotatebox{225.0}{\line{1}{0}{6.0}}}\end{picture}
, respectively, where
k
is the context’s depth.
We have
t_{1}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s_{1}
by the rule’s condition.
By the induction
hypothesis,
t_{1}\theta\allowbreak\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s_{1}\theta
.
Thus, either
t^{\prime}=s^{\prime}

or, by Theorem
8
,
t^{\prime}\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s^{\prime}
.

Rules
3
,
4
,
5
:
We have
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
. If
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{A}>\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s){\big|}_{A}
,
rule
1
applies as above. Otherwise,
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t){\big|}_{A}=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s){\big|}_{A}
. If
t\theta=s\theta
, there is nothing to prove.
Otherwise, the corresponding rule
2
,

3
, or
4
applies. The rest of the proof is as
for
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
above.

The converse of Theorem
25
does not hold.
However, it does hold on ground preterms:

Theorem 26
.

The relation
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
coincides with
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
on ground preterms.

Proof 4.5
.

One direction of the equivalence follows by
Theorem
25
. It remains to show that
t\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
implies
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
. The proof is by induction on the definition of

\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
. It is easy to see that to every case in the definition of
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

corresponds a case in the definition of
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
. As for the weights,
\cal W_{\mathsf{g}}
and
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}
coincide. In particular, for a ground preterm, the polynomial
returned by
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}
contains no indeterminates.
To account for the mismatch between
\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and
\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
,
we apply Lemma
22
.

Theorem 27
.

The relation
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
coincides with
\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
on ground preterms.

Proof 4.6
.

One direction of the equivalence follows by
Theorem
25
. It remains to show that
t\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
implies
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
. If
t=s
, Lemma
22
applies. Otherwise, we appeal to
Theorem
26
to obtain
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.
By Lemma
23
, this implies
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

Lemma 28
.

If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, then
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
.

Proof 4.7
.

We proceed by structural induction on
t
.

For all rules except rule
2
, the claim is obvious.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
was derived by rule
2
,
then
t
is of the form
y\>\bar{t}
and
s
is of the form
y\>\bar{s}
,
and for every
i
,
t_{i}
is steady and
t_{i}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s_{i}
.
By the induction hypothesis,
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t_{i})\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s_{i})
for every
i
.
Let
\upsilon_{i}
be the type of
t_{i}
, which is also the type of
s_{i}
.
Let
n
be the length of
\bar{t}
, which is also the length of
\bar{s}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)

\displaystyle=1+\mathbf{w}_{y}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}_{y,i}(\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}({t}_{i})-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})

\displaystyle\geq 1+\mathbf{w}_{y}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{k}_{y,i}(\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}({s}_{i})-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})

\displaystyle=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)

Theorem 29
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
and
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, then
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.
If in addition
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
or
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, then even
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

Proof 4.8
.

We proceed by well-founded induction on the multiset
\{|u|,|t|,|s|\}
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
or
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
was derived by rule
1
,
then rule
1
yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
by transitivity of weight comparison
and Lemma
28
.
So, for the remainder of this proof, we may assume that
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(u)=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)=\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
was derived by rule
2
,
then
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
must have been derived by rule
2
, too.
Then rule
2
also yields
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s

by the induction hypothesis.
Since all three preterms
u,s,t
are headed by variables, neither

u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
nor
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
hold, and thus we need not prove

u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
was derived by rule
3a
or
3b
,
then
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
must be derived by rule
3
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
was derived by rule
3a
or
3b

as well, then rule
3a
or
3b

also yield
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
by the induction hypothesis and
by transitivity of
the strict first-order KBO
\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
on
\cal T(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\emptyset)
.
If in addition
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
or
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
,
then this must be by rule
2a
or
2b
.
Then rule
2a
or
2b

yield
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
by the induction hypothesis and by transitivity of
\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
was derived by rule
3c
,
then rule
2c
yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
was derived by rule
3c
,
then
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
must be derived by rule
4
or
5
.
Then
2c
yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
was derived by rule
4a
or
4b
,
then
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
must be derived by rule
4
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
was derived by rule
4a
or
4b

as well, then rule
4a
or
4b

also yield
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s

by transitivity of
>
on natural numbers
and by the induction hypothesis, which implies transitivity of
\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
on the relevant preterms.
If in addition
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
or
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
,
then this must be by rule
3a
or
3b
.
Then rule
3a
or
3b

yield
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
by
by transitivity of
>
on natural numbers and the induction hypothesis.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
was derived by rule

,
then rule
3c
yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

was derived by rule

,
then

must be derived by rule

.
Then rule

yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
was derived by rule
5
,
then

must be derived by rule

, too.
Then rule

also yields
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
by transitivity
of the precedence

,
by transitivity of
\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
and its lexicographic extension,
and by the induction hypothesis, which implies transitivity of
\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
on the relevant preterms.
If in addition
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}t
or
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
,
then this must be by rule
4
.
Then rule

yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
by transitivity
of the precedence

,

\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
and its lexicographic extension,
and by the induction hypothesis.

Theorem 30
.

Let
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
.
Let
\theta
be a substitution such that all variables in

t\theta
and
s\theta
are nonfunctional.
Let
s\theta
contain a nonfunctional variable
x

outside of parameters.
Then
t\theta
must also contain
x
outside of parameters.

Proof 4.9
.

Since
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s
, we have
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s)
.
By Lemma
24
, we have
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t\theta)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s\theta)
.
By definition of
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}
,
since all variables in
s\theta
are nonfunctional,

W_{\mathsf{m}}(s\theta)
must contain
\mathbf{w}_{x}
with a nonzero coefficient.
Since
\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(t\theta)\geq\cal W_{\mathsf{m}}(s\theta)
,

t\theta
must also contain
\mathbf{w}_{x}
with a nonzero coefficient.
Therefore,
x
must occur outside of parameters in
t\theta
.

4.2.
\bm{\lambda}
LPO

{defi}

Let
>^{\mathsf{ty}}
be a precedence on
\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}}
.
Let
\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
be the strict first-order LPO on
\cal T(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\emptyset)

induced by
>^{\mathsf{ty}}
.
Let
>
be a precedence on
\Sigma
.
Let
\mathsf{ws}\in\Sigma
be the watershed.

The
strict monomorphic
\lambda
LPO

\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
and the
nonstrict
monomorphic
\lambda
LPO

\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
induced by
>^{\mathsf{ty}},>
on

\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak\emptyset,\allowbreak X)
are defined by mutual induction. The strict
relation is defined so that
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
if any of these conditions is met:

(1)

t
is of the form
\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\upsilon}\rangle(\bar{w})\>\bar{t}_{k}
and
any of these conditions is met:

(a)

t_{i}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, or

(b)

s=\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
,
\mathsf{g}>\mathsf{f}
, and

\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})
, or

(c)

s=\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\tau}\rangle(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
,
\bar{\upsilon}\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{ty}}\bar{\tau}
,
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})
, or

(d)

s=\mathsf{g}\langle\bar{\upsilon}\rangle(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
,

(\bar{w},\bar{t})\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}(\bar{u},\bar{s})
,
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})
, or

(e)

\mathsf{g}>\mathsf{ws}
and

s
is either of the form
m\>\bar{s}
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})

or of the form
\lambda\>s^{\prime}
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,[s^{\prime}])
;

(2)

t
is of the form
n\>\bar{t}_{k}
and any of these conditions is met:

(a)

t_{i}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
for some
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, or

(b)

s=m\>\bar{s}
,
n>m
, and
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})
, or

(c)

s=n\>\bar{s}
,
\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\bar{s}
, and

\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})
, or

(d)

s
is of the form
\lambda\>s^{\prime}
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,[s^{\prime}])
or
of the form or
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
,
where
\mathsf{f}\leq\mathsf{ws}
,
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})
;

(3)

t
is of the form
\lambda\langle\upsilon\rangle\>t^{\prime}
and any of these
conditions is met:

(a)

t^{\prime}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
, or

(b)

s=\lambda\langle\tau\rangle\>s^{\prime}
,
\upsilon\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}\tau
, and

\cal{chkargs}(t,[s^{\prime}])
, or

(c)

s=\lambda\langle\upsilon\rangle\>s^{\prime}
and
t^{\prime}\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s^{\prime}
, or

(d)

s
is of the form
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{s}
,
where
\mathsf{f}\leq\mathsf{ws}
,
and
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s})

where
\cal{chkargs}(t,\bar{s}_{k})
if and only if
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s_{i}
for every
i\in\{1,\dots,k\}
.
The nonstrict relation is defined so that
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
if any of these

t

where

is defined as above.

The only syntactic differences between the definitions of
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
and
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}

are
that
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
uses
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
instead of
\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}

and
\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
instead of
\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
.
Moreover, rule
1
of
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
is analogous to
rule
2
in the definition of
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
. As for rules

2
–
4
of
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
, they are nearly
identical to the rules defining the strict orders
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
and
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
.

Analogous theorems to those about
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
also hold about

\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
and
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
.

Lemma 31
.

s\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
for every monomorphic preterm
s
.

Proof 4.10
.

By straightforward induction on
s
.

Lemma 32
.

If
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
, then
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.

Proof 4.11
.

By induction on the derivation of
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
For each rule, there is a clearly corresponding rule of
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
to apply.
The induction hypothesis is only required for applying rule
4c
.
A crucial observation is that
\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
implies
\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
by definition.

Theorem 33
.

If
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
, then
t\theta\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s\theta
for any grounding substitution

\theta
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
, then
t\theta\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s\theta
for any grounding
substitution
\theta
.

Proof 4.12
.

The proof of the two claims is by induction on the shape of the derivation of
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
and
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
As in the proof of Theorem
25
,
we observe that

–

\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\bar{s}
implies
\bar{t}\theta\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\bar{s}\theta
, and

–

\bar{t}\mathrel{{\succapprox}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=1.72218pt,depth=3.01385pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succapprox}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\bar{s}
implies

\bar{t}\theta\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}\bar{s}\theta
or

\bar{t}\theta=\bar{s}\theta
.

For the first claim,
we make a case distinction on the rule deriving
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
In each case, the corresponding rule of
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
applies.
The only mismatches between the two definitions are
the use of
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
versus
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and

\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
versus
\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
.
These are repaired by the induction
hypothesis and our observation above.

For the second claim,
we make a case distinction on the rule deriving
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
:

Rule
1
:
Analogous to the case for rule
2
of
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
in the proof of
Theorem
25
.

Rules
2
,
3
,
4
:
If
t\theta=s\theta
, there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, the corresponding
rule
1
,
2
, or
3
applies. The
rest of the proof is as for
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
.

Theorem 34
.

The relation
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
coincides with
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
on ground preterms.

Proof 4.13
.

One direction of the equivalence follows by
Theorem
33
. It remains to show that
t\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
implies
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
. The proof is by induction on the definition of

\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
. It is easy to see that to every case in the definition of
\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}

corresponds a case in the definition of
\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
.
To account for the mismatches
between
\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}
and
and
between
\mathrel{\succ\!\!\!\!\!\succ}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
and
\mathrel{{\succsim}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss\color[rgb]{1,1,1}\vrule height=0.86108pt,depth=2.58334pt,width=7.3194pt}\!\!\!\!\!{\succsim}}^{\smash{\mathsf{l}}\mathsf{ex}}_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
,
we apply Lemmas
31
and
32
.

Theorem 35
.

The relation
\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
coincides with
\succeq_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}
on ground preterms.

Proof 4.14
.

One direction of the equivalence follows by
Theorem
33
. It remains to show that
t\succ_{\mathsf{g\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
implies
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
. If
t=s
, Lemma
31
applies. Otherwise, we appeal to
Theorem
34
to obtain
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
By Lemma
32
, this implies
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.

Theorem 36
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}t
and
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
, then
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
If in addition
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}t
or
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
, then even
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.

Proof 4.15
.

We proceed by well-founded induction on the multiset
\{|u|,|t|,|s|\}
.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}t
was derived by rule
1
,
then

must have been derived by rule
1
, too.
Then rule
1
also yields
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s

by the induction hypothesis.

If

2a
,

3a
, or
4a
,

by rule
1a
,

2a
, or
3a
and the induction hypothesis.

If
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}t
was derived by rule
2b
,

2c
, or
2d
,
then

must have been derived by rule

, too.

was derived by rule
2a
,
then the
\cal{chkargs}
-condition and the induction hypothesis
yield
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
was derived by rule
2b
,

2c
, or
2d
,
then rule
2b
,

2c
, or

also yield
u\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s

by transitivity
of the precedence
>
,
by transitivity of

and its lexicographic extension,
and by the induction hypothesis, which implies

on the relevant preterms
and the required
\cal{chkargs}
-condition.
If moreover

,

by

,

, or

.

by
rule
1e

using transitivity of the precedence

and the induction hypothesis.

or

-condition and the induction hypothesis
yield
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
If
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
was derived by rule
2b
,

2c
, or
2d
,
then rule
3d

yields
u\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
,
using transitivity of the precedence
>
and the induction hypothesis.

Theorem 37
.

Let
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
or
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
Let
\theta
be a substitution such that all variables in

t\theta
and
s\theta
are nonfunctional.
Let
s\theta
contain a nonfunctional variable
x

outside of parameters.
Then
t\theta
must also contain
x
outside of parameters.

Proof 4.16
.

The proof of the two claims is by induction on the shape of the derivation of
t\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
or
t\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s
.
In most cases, the claims follow directly form the induction hypothesis.
For case
1
, we have
t=y\>\bar{t}
and
s=y\>\bar{s}

and for every
i
,
t_{i}
is steady and
t_{i}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s_{i}
.
Our assumption is that
s\theta
contains a nonfunctional variable
x

outside of parameters.
The
x
could originate from
y\theta
or from
s_{i}\theta
for some
i
.
If it originates from
y\theta
, then
x
must also occur outside of parameters
in
t\theta=y\theta\>\bar{t}\theta

because
t_{i}
is steady for all
i
.
If it originates from
s_{i}\theta
, then
x
must also occur in
t_{i}\theta
outside of parameters
by the induction hypothesis because
t_{i}\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptlpo}}s_{i}
.
Since
t_{i}
is steady,
x
must also occur in
t\theta
outside of parameters.

5.
The Polymorphic Level

In a third and final step, we generalize the definition of
\lambda
KBO and

\lambda
LPO to polymorphic nonground preterms. The resulting orders coincide with
the monomorphic nonground
\lambda
KBO and
\lambda
LPO on monomorphic preterms
while supporting type variables.

Type variables, in conjunction with the
\eta
-long
\beta
-normal form, lead to
substantial complications. Instantiating a type variable with a functional type
causes
\eta
-expansion to take place, transforming for instance

y\langle\alpha\rangle
into
\lambda\>y\langle\beta\to\gamma\rangle\>0

or even
\lambda\>\lambda\>y\langle\beta\to(\beta\to\beta)\to\gamma\rangle\>1\>(\lambda\>1\>0)
.
This affects the weight calculation of
\lambda
KBO, since each
\eta
-expansion
increases the weight by
\cal w_{\lambda}+\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}
. Our solution is to add a
term to the polynomial to account for possible
\eta
-expansion. This also
affects the shape comparison of
\lambda
KBO and
\lambda
LPO, since the shape
of any preterm whose type is a type variable
\alpha
can change radically as a
result of instantiating
\alpha
.

If we used the
\eta
-short
\beta
-normal form instead, we would be out of the
frying pan into the fire. Applying a
\lambda
-abstraction to an argument makes
not only the
\lambda
but also De Bruijn indices disappear. Applying an

\eta
-reduced functional term
t
to an argument, however, makes neither a

\lambda
nor De Bruijn indices disappear, resulting in a weight discrepancy of
at least
\cal w_{\lambda}
compared with a
\lambda
-abstraction of the same type
and weight. Moreover, the
\eta
-short normal form makes it more difficult, if
not impossible, to achieve another of our goals, namely, the order requirement
for the
\mathsf{diff}
symbol of the optimistic
\lambda
-superposition calculus.

5.1.
\bm{\lambda}
KBO

{defi}

Let
(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\Sigma)
be a higher-order signature.
We denote by
\mathbf{P}
the set of
\mathbf{O}
-valued polynomials of the indeterminates

\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bar{t}}
,
\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bar{t},i}
, and
\mathbf{h}_{\alpha}
. The
first two are the same as in Definition
4.1
,
except that preterms are now polymorphic. The last one is as follows,
where
\alpha\in X_{\mathsf{ty}}
:

–

\mathbf{h}_{\alpha}
, ranging over
\mathbb{N}
, represents the number of

\eta
-expansions incurred as a result of instantiating
\alpha

for one preterm of type
\alpha
(excluding any subterms).

Auxiliary concepts are defined as in
Definition
4.1
.

For example, if
\mathsf{c}:\alpha
and
\alpha\theta=(\kappa\to\kappa)\to\kappa
, then
\mathsf{c}\theta=\lambda\>\mathsf{c}\>(\lambda\>1\>0)
.
In this case, instantiation caused two
\eta
-expansions, including one to
a De Bruijn index.

{defi}

Let
\Sigma^{\prime}=\Sigma\uplus\{k\mid k\in\mathbf{O}_{>0}\}
with

k:\Pi\alpha.\>\alpha
.
Define the normalization function
\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\phantom{i}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}:\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X)\to\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma^{\prime},\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X)

recursively by

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}y\>\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=y\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}k\langle\tau\rangle\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}&\text{if $\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)=1$ for every $i$, with $\cal w(\mathsf{f})=k$ and $\mathsf{f}(\bar{u}):\tau$}\\
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}m\langle\tau\rangle\>\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}\langle\tau\rangle\>\bm{[\kern 0.83328pt}\bar{t}\bm{\kern 0.83328pt]}

\displaystyle\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}\lambda\>t\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

\displaystyle=\lambda\>\bm{[\kern 0.95827pt}t\bm{\kern 0.95827pt]}

{defi}

Define the
\eta
-expansion polynomial
\cal H:\cal{Ty}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}})\to\mathbf{P}
by

\displaystyle\cal H(\alpha)

\displaystyle=(\cal w_{\lambda}+\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})\mathbf{h}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle\cal H(\kappa(\bar{\tau}))

\displaystyle=0

{defi}

Let
\cal w:\Sigma\to\mathbf{O}_{>0}
,
\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}\in\mathbf{O}_{>0}
,
and
\cal k:\Sigma\times\mathbb{N}_{>0}\to\mathbf{O}_{>0}
.
For every
\mathsf{f}\in\Sigma
and
i>\cal{arity}(\mathsf{f})
, we require

(\mathrm{K})

\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)=1
.
Given a list of preterms
\bar{t}
, let
\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}
denote the longest suffix
consisting of steady preterms, and let
\bar{t}^{\>\star}
denote the complementary
prefix.
Define the polymorphic weight function
\cal W:\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X)\to\mathbf{P}
recursively by

\displaystyle\cal W(y\>\bar{t})

\displaystyle=1+\mathbf{w}_{y\>\bm{[\kern 0.67078pt}\bar{t}^{\>\star}\bm{\kern 0.67078pt]}}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{|\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}|}\mathbf{k}_{y\>\bm{[\kern 0.67078pt}\bar{t}^{\>\star}\bm{\kern 0.67078pt]},i}(\cal W(\bar{t}^{\>\star\!\star}_{i})-\cal w_{\mathsf{db}})+\cal H(\tau)

if
y\>\bar{t}:\tau

\displaystyle\cal W(\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}_{n})

\displaystyle=\cal w(\mathsf{f})+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal k(\mathsf{f},i)\cal W(t_{i})+\cal H(\tau)

if
\mathsf{f}(\bar{u})\>\bar{t}_{n}:\tau

\displaystyle\cal W(m\>\bar{t}_{n})

\displaystyle=\cal w_{\mathsf{db}}+\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{n}\cal W(t_{i})+\cal H(\tau)

if
m\>\bar{t}_{n}:\tau

\displaystyle\cal W(\lambda\>t)

\displaystyle=\cal w_{\lambda}+\cal W(t)

Notice, in the definition above, the presence of
\cal H(\tau)
monomials to
account for
\eta
-expansion caused by type variable instantiation.

{defi}

Let
\tau,\upsilon\in\cal{Ty}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}})
. The polymorphism comparison

\upsilon\unrhd\tau
holds if
\tau
is not a type variable or if

\upsilon=\tau
.
Moreover, let
s,t\in\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma,\allowbreak\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X,\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}})
such that

s:\tau
,
t:\upsilon
. We write
t\unrhd s
if
\upsilon\unrhd\tau
.

{defi}

Let
\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}},\cal w,\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}},\cal k,\allowbreak\cal W

be as in Definition
5.1
.
Let
>^{\mathsf{ty}}
be a precedence on
\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}}
.
Let
\succ_{\mathsf{ty}}
be the strict first-order KBO on
\cal T(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},X_{\mathsf{ty}})

\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}}
and

.
Let
>
be a precedence on
\Sigma
.

The
strict polymorphic
\lambda
KBO

\succ_{\mathsf{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}},\cal w,\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}},\cal k,\allowbreak>^{\mathsf{ty}},>

,

, or

\succ_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

,

3a
,

3c
,

4a

and

holds.

The

\lambda

\succsim_{\mathsf{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

\cal w_{\mathsf{ty}},\cal w,\cal w_{\lambda},\cal w_{\mathsf{db}},\cal k,\allowbreak>^{\mathsf{ty}},>

on
\cal T^{\infty}_{\mathrm{pre}}(\Sigma_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak\Sigma,\allowbreak X_{\mathsf{ty}},\allowbreak X)

t\succsim_{\mathsf{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}s

,

3a
,

3b
,

4b
, or

\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

the rule

,

,

4c

, or

\succsim_{\mathsf{m\raisebox{0.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-1.05]{${}_{\mathsf{y}}$}}\kern-0.5833ptkbo}}

and

holds.

For some of the rules, the condition

is necessary to compare

and

. For the other rules, the condition can be derived from the types of

and
